## Motion Authoring, Editing & Retargeting

- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.
- 2026-10-28, **Decompose, Don't Expand: Capability-Independent AI Systems by Construction**, Tejaswi Gowda et.al., Paper: [@2610.38144](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38144)
> **Abstract:** A recurring design choice in building AI systems is whether, when a task is too hard for a model, to expand the model (make it larger, better-prompted, fine-tuned), or to decompose the task (extract the parts that admit a deterministic solution to a host, leaving the model only the irreducibly fuzzy residue). We make three claims. First, that many tasks commonly delegated whole to a model are in fact deterministic tasks with a small generative core. Second, and our central empirical result, that decomposing such a task yields a measurable and desirable property we call parity by construction: on the extracted sub-tasks the model-size performance curve becomes flat, because no model performs them at all. We measure this across nine models spanning a 494M-parameter local checkpoint to a frontier Opus-class model, a thirty-fold parameter range on the local family alone: once the host resolves the context, operation selection scores identically for all nine models and selector resolution for eight of nine, while the one task that does not decompose, labelling, remains sloped across the same range. Because the task leaves the model, a stock checkpoint suffices: no task-specific training and no fine-tuning. Third, that this is a general design discipline, not a domain trick: we show the same move recurring across physics setup, motion retargeting, and object-model editing, and in a Web-CLI tool family (same author, so not an independent replication). We give a method for locating the cut, the construction that makes a cut exist (a deterministic surface and a closed grammar over it), and a way to make the residue safe (resolve the deterministic context; verify the generative output), position the work against neuro-symbolic AI, tool-augmented language models, and generate-and-verify approaches, and are explicit about what is measured (one domain) versus illustrative (the rest).

## Character Motion Synthesis & Animation

- 2026-11-05, **EgoGeneralist: Unifying Egocentric Action-Video Tasks through Video-Native Modeling**, Yanlin Jin et.al., Paper: [@2611.08477](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08477)
> **Abstract:** Modern video models possess strong visual priors, yet do not natively represent human action in metric 3D space. We present EgoGeneralist, which adapts pretrained video generators to unify action-conditioned egocentric video generation, pose estimation, and joint action-video generation. Our role-adaptive action renderer provides video-native representations for multidirectional action-video learning, making metric motion usable as both a spatial control signal and a recoverable generation target. Cross-stream action-video coupling establishes close correspondence between metric motion and visual dynamics through lightweight adaptation while preserving the pretrained visual prior. Together, these designs establish a generalist framework spanning five action-video tasks. Despite adaptation only on controlled laboratory recordings, EgoGeneralist generalizes to diverse unseen scenes and interactions with effective action control and visual quality, bridging pretrained visual knowledge and metric 3D human motion.
- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.
- 2026-11-05, **IMUDINO: Self-distilled Foundation Model for Inertial Sensing**, Zhoutong Ye et.al., Paper: [@2611.07437](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07437)
> **Abstract:** We present IMUDINO, the first foundation model (FM) for inertial sensing to achieve competitive performance across diverse sensing tasks including human activity recognition (HAR), health, motion capture, and odometry. Prior inertial FMs often focus on one subfield of inertial sensing and sometimes underperform state-of-the-art supervised methods (e.g., in HAR), making their scope considerably narrower than FMs for vision and natural language. Moreover, they often require fixed input length and sampling rate, and generalize poorly to unseen sensor locations. To build a general-purpose foundation model for inertial sensing, we adapt the DINO self-supervised learning (SSL) paradigm to inertial data through a set of semantics-preserving motion transformations that simulate variations in motion pattern and sensor setup. For model architecture, we use a plain transformer encoder with frequency-aware positional encoding that natively supports varying input length and sampling rate. This simple setup proved remarkably effective for learning global and dense features generalizable across users, sensor setups, tasks, and, in the case of robot odometry, embodiments. We hope IMUDINO can serve as a backbone for inertial sensing and kickstart the development of general-purpose inertial FMs.
- 2026-11-04, **Semantic Residual Vector Quantization for Sign Language Generation**, Chang Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.06781](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06781)
> **Abstract:** Sign language generation (SLG) maps written sentences into signing motion while preserving both lexical content and fine-grained articulation. This requires a model to decide what signs should be expressed and to realize them through precise handshape, trajectory, facial expression and body coordination. Existing pose-based systems and reconstruction-only motion tokenizers often entangle these two decisions, leaving the generator without an explicit text-aligned intermediate target for what to sign before refining how to sign it. To address this issue, we introduce Semantic Residual Vector Quantization (SRVQ), a framework for SLG that frames generation as a two-level process moving from semantics to kinematics. The newly introduced SRV Quantizer learns a hierarchy in which the first residual level encodes sign content and subsequent levels refine pose, handshape, trajectory, and body coordination, all within a single quantizer and without separate semantic representations or external planning stages. Through word-aware conditioning for hierarchical code generation, a masked semantic generator predicts semantic codes and a kinematic generator completes the residual codes for full SMPL-X motion synthesis. We evaluate using retrieval and FID in a shared text-motion embedding space, alongside standard geometric metrics. On CSL-Daily and Phoenix-2014T, SRVQ achieves R@1 of 82.3 and 70.3, outperforming the previous SOTA by 30 and 46 points, respectively.
- 2026-11-04, **Human Motion Prediction via Continual Prior Compensation**, Jianwei Tang et.al., Paper: [@2611.06637](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06637)
> **Abstract:** Human Motion Prediction (HMP) aims to predict future human poses at different moments according to observed past motion sequences. Previous approaches mainly treated the prediction of different temporal moments as a single prediction task and learned the predictions of varied moments simultaneously, which would encounter a main limitation: the learning of short-term predictions (referring to ``near-future'' prediction) could be hindered by the predictions of long-term (referring to ``far-future'' prediction) motions. In this paper, we develop a novel temporal continual learning framework called Continual Prior Compensation (CPC) to progressively train HMP models, in which we divide the prediction task of motions corresponding to varied temporal moments into several subtasks and train the model in a multi-stage manner. To mitigate the prior information forgetting in the progressive training, we further introduce a learnable random variable Prior Compensation Factor (PCF) to explicitly measure the prior knowledge loss. We theoretically show that the PCF can be efficiently learned together with the model parameters by minimizing a reasonable upper bound of the objective function. The proposed CPC is further enhanced to estimate the prior information loss for each subtask and a new framework called Continual Prior Compensation++ (CPC++) with Fine-Grained Prior Compensation Factor (FGPCF) is finally developed. Our CPC and CPC++ frameworks are quite flexible and can be easily integrated with different HMP backbone models and adapted to various datasets and applications. Extensive experiments on three HMP benchmark datasets using multiple SOTA HMP backbones (PGBIG, siMLPe, MotionMixer, and LTD) demonstrate the effectiveness and flexibility of our frameworks.
- 2026-11-03, **Möbius: A Unified Motion-Language Model Bridging Understanding and Generation**, Jiazhi Shu et.al., Paper: [@2611.05279](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05279)
> **Abstract:** We aim to build a truly unified motion model that can understand, generate, and edit human motion within a single framework. Existing motion UMMs remain only partially unified: motion understanding is handled by a language model, while generation is often delegated to a separate diffusion module operating over learned motion latents. We introduce Möbius, which brings the entire diffusion process into the same multimodal representation space used for motion understanding. Clean motion, noisy motion, and text jointly participate in shared attention, while role-specific parameters process understanding and generation streams. Importantly, Möbius operates directly on continuous frame-level raw motion, requiring neither discrete tokenization nor a pretrained motion VAE. This simple formulation naturally supports motion captioning, text-to-motion generation, temporal completion, and instruction-guided editing within a single model. On HumanML3D, Möbius achieves state-of-the-art performance among unified motion models. Scaling to 1.7B parameters and 1,700 hours of motion data further demonstrates that our framework is both effective and scalable.
- 2026-11-01, **MoPort: A View-Aware 2D Interface for Motion Language Models**, Lixing Tan et.al., Paper: [@2611.01541](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01541)
> **Abstract:** Motion language models (MoLMs) encode 3D human motion as discrete tokens for language-based understanding. Deploying them on monocular video raises two problems. Pose detection yields 2D joint sequences, leaving a dimensional gap from the 3D motion used in training. Moreover, the 2D observation changes with the camera viewpoint, while the depth lost in projection makes the 2D pose ambiguous across viewpoints. We propose MoPort, a view-aware 2D interface that enables MoLMs to take 2D observations as input. We introduce Paired View Alignment (PVA), which gives two views of the same motion a shared 3D teacher target and directly constrains the consistency of their latents, so that 2D observations from different viewpoints are mapped to a consistent semantic representation. Camera-Modulated Encoding (CME) estimates the camera extrinsics from the 2D sequence and uses them to modulate encoding, providing the 2D latents with geometric information about the viewpoint. MoPort achieves R@1 of 0.5674 on HumanML3D and 0.3286 on KIT-ML, outperforming all evaluated baselines. On HumanML3D, its R@1 decreases only from 0.5763 on in-distribution viewpoints to 0.5585 on out-of-distribution viewpoints. We validate portability on three downstream MoLMs. Furthermore, on the real-world RGB video dataset Fit3D, MoPort achieves 80.36\% accuracy. Code will be released upon acceptance.
- 2026-10-30, **Movens-Zero: Scaling Humanoid Motion Foundation Models with Large-Scale Human Videos**, Wendong Bu et.al., Paper: [@2610.41877](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41877)
> **Abstract:** Scaling humanoid motion foundation models is constrained by the scarcity of diverse, robot-executable data and by the difficulty of jointly learning from heterogeneous multimodal inputs while supporting real-time closed-loop control. We present Movens-5M, constructed from raw Internet videos through clip generation, quality filtering, semantic annotation, motion recovery, and physical refinement with humanoid retargeting and execution verification. It contains 15 million human-motion sequences and 5 million physically executable humanoid-motion sequences spanning over 10,000 hours, aligned with text, RGB video, speech, and music. Building on this data, we introduce Movens-Zero, a scalable understanding-generation-execution architecture that supports joint learning across modalities and real-time closed-loop execution. Task-mixed flow-matching training jointly optimizes a single action expert across seven generation tasks, enabling direct motion generation from text, speech, music, and video. Multimodal Real-Time Chunking connects overlapping chunks under continuously updated instructions and robot states for continuous closed-loop execution. We further introduce Movens-Intent, a four-track benchmark with 8,192 expert-verified condition-motion pairs across text, speech, music, and video. Experiments show consistent gains across all four modalities when scaling execution-verified data from 1% to 100% and action-expert capacity from 64M to 911M parameters, while demonstrating that data quality and cross-modal supervision improve generation and execution.
- 2026-10-29, **TalkFlow: Structured Latent Flow Matching for Speech Driven Holistic Motion Generation**, Xinmu Wang et.al., Paper: [@2610.40972](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40972)
> **Abstract:** Holistic co-speech motion generation aims to synthesize coordinated facial expressions, hand gestures, and body movements from speech, which is challenging due to heterogeneous part-specific dynamics, long-range temporal coherence, and cross-part coordination. Directly modeling high-dimensional motion can be unstable, while overly compact or unstructured latent spaces may lose fine-grained motion details. We present \textbf{TalkFlow}, a structured latent flow framework for speech-driven holistic motion generation. TalkFlow learns a continuous three-stream latent representation for upper-body motion, lower-body motion, and facial expressions, preserving part-specific dynamics without forcing heterogeneous motion components into a single latent space. To maintain holistic coordination, lightweight cross-stream interactions are incorporated in both latent representation learning and flow-based generation. Conditioned on multi-level speech features, TalkFlow performs speech-conditioned latent flow matching, learning a continuous transport process from source latents to realistic motion latents. Experiments on the BEAT2 dataset demonstrate strong performance across facial expression accuracy, body gesture quality, and speech-motion synchronization. Qualitative comparisons and user studies further show that TalkFlow generates more natural, expressive, and speech-aligned holistic motion than state-of-the-art methods. The project page is available at https://xinmuwang.github.io/TalkFlow/.
- 2026-10-28, **ETA-Motion: Efficient Topology-Agnostic Motion Diffusion via Sparse Joint Learning**, Seoyoung Hwang et.al., Paper: [@2610.38259](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38259)
> **Abstract:** Topology-agnostic motion generation aims to synthesize motions for diverse characters with arbitrary skeletons. Unlike conventional motion generation, which assumes a fixed skeletal template, this setting must handle diverse joint configurations and inconsistent joint semantics across characters. Existing methods address this challenge through dense full-joint modeling, which incurs substantial computational cost and hinders learning transferable motion patterns across diverse skeletal topologies. We present ETA-Motion, a topology-agnostic motion generation framework based on sparse joint learning. Instead of learning motion from all joints, our method uses a compact subset of joints to reduce redundancy and trains the motion generator on the resulting sparse skeleton. To facilitate correspondence across diverse topologies, we introduce semantic joint embeddings that combine textual and visual cues, together with topology-aware training objectives for preserving skeletal structure. Although ETA-Motion is trained only on sparse skeletal observations, it supports full-skeleton motion generation during inference. Experimental results demonstrate that ETA-Motion outperforms existing topology-agnostic baselines while significantly improving efficiency and generalization to unseen motions and unseen topologies.
- 2026-10-28, **StepControl: Long-Horizon Human Motion Generation from Foot-Contact Schedules**, Hojun Jang et.al., Paper: [@2610.38153](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38153)
> **Abstract:** Controllable human motion generation has largely focused on specifying what action should occur or where the body should move. We study a complementary question: can full-body motion be controlled by specifying only when each foot contacts the ground? We introduce StepControl, an autoregressive diffusion model conditioned on a future binary left/right foot-contact schedule, without requiring target root and body poses, or semantic prompts. StepControl represents contact as explicit temporal events and injects them through dedicated cross-attention with classifier-free guidance. We further introduce a stochastic mixture noise schedule and warmup strategy for stable long-horizon autoregressive generation. Experiments show that StepControl follows prescribed contact timing more accurately than adapting existing spatially guided motion generators to contact constraints. It maintains effective contact control over long continuous rollouts and responds to edited schedules such as cadence changes, stop-and-go pauses, limping gaits, and bilateral jumping. Our results show that minimal foot-contact schedules provide an effective and intuitive temporal interface for controllable motion generation.

## Vision-Language & Action Models (VLA/VLM)

- 2026-11-06, **On Agentic Manipulation Policies**, Chen Chen et.al., Paper: [@2611.09892](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09892)
> **Abstract:** Recent work on robot manipulation has focused on specialist policies trained on demonstrations. We show that generalist AI models can control manipulators more reliably than specialist policies such as vision-language-action models (VLAs) and world-action models (WAMs). We systematically analyze agentic manipulation policies (AMPs), in which a generalist AI model commands the manipulator directly, adapting its actions as the task unfolds. We present AMP1, a reference harness that runs unchanged across single-arm, bimanual, and mobile manipulation. AMP1 achieves state-of-the-art results on the RoboLab-120 and CaP-Gym benchmarks, with no demonstrations or task-specific training. On RoboLab-120, AMP1 reaches an 85.6\% success rate zero-shot, compared to 49.2\% and 42.9\% for leading VLA and WAM policies. The agent succeeds zero-shot in 104 of 108 real-world trials spanning twelve tasks and three robot instances, reducing failure rates by 20x and 15x relative to strong VLA and WAM baselines. The basic harness supplies no grasping, pushing, or placing skills; the agent develops its own strategies, such as searching for hidden objects, using objects as tools, and calibrating its cameras. With the addition of a persistent memory store, the agent can also learn from experience: an augmented harness with a memory representation that is carried across episodes lowers the failure rate on RoboDojo bimanual manipulation tasks by 4.6x relative to zero-shot deployment. Our analysis suggests that AMPs are substantially more general and robust than specialist manipulation policies, providing a promising foundation for robot manipulation.
- 2026-11-06, **BEV3SR: Evidence-Aware 3D Spatial Reasoning from Video**, Fan Zhu et.al., Paper: [@2611.09816](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09816)
> **Abstract:** 3D spatial reasoning from video remains challenging for VLMs because videos provide sequential, view-local observations rather than persistent metric scene records. Many questions must be answered in a query-specified perspective rather than the observed camera viewpoint, and prompting with a fixed set of RGB views may miss sparse or occluded visual cues. To address these gaps, we introduce BEV3SR, a training-free and backbone-agnostic framework for RGB-D videos with camera parameters. BEV3SR turns video observations into queryable metric bird's-eye-view (BEV) scene memory and formalizes each query into an evidence-aware spatial task. The Spatial Task Formalizer specifies the query's reference frame, spatial constraints, objective, and evidence requirement. The evidence requirement determines the appropriate reasoning pathway. A query treated as requiring geometric evidence is handled by the Geometric Reasoner, which deterministically computes the answer using the metric BEV records in the query-specified reference frame. Otherwise, a query treated as requiring visual evidence is passed to the Visual Evidence Aligner, which uses BEV-indexed object records and their associated supporting views to retrieve object-consistent and scene-context RGB frames for VLM grounding. Within a standard 16-frame budget, BEV3SR consistently improves different VLM backbones across multiple 3D scene QA benchmarks, delivering large gains on spatially intensive questions and significant improvements on open-ended scene QA. These results demonstrate that BEV3SR offers an effective, efficient, and interpretable framework for explicit 3D spatial reasoning from video.
- 2026-11-06, **Seeing What Matters: Benchmarking Task-Conditioned Actionable Spatial Belief for Manipulation**, Yina Jian et.al., Paper: [@2611.09794](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09794)
> **Abstract:** When should a robot look at the cup, and when at the portafilter? Spatial-reasoning benchmarks evaluate whether a model can describe a scene, but manipulation needs something more specific: the ability to acquire the particular evidence that makes the current command executable. A view that is informative for one task can be useless for another, even at the same workstation. We introduce Seeing What Matters, a benchmark for task-conditioned actionable spatial belief in simulated tabletop manipulation. Across three domains--coffee workstation, drawer/cabinet, and microwave/appliance--the benchmark provides 860 frozen evaluation cases; expanding across methods, budgets, and inference repeats yields more than 115,000 evaluator episodes under two protocols: closed-loop actionability and same-object functional out-of-distribution generalization. Each case pairs a set of candidate RGB views with a manipulation command, shared scripted skills, and hidden postconditions, so the benchmark directly measures whether a view-selection policy acquires the observations its command actually needs. We also provide a diagnostic reference pipeline that maintains a spatial-functional belief state and ranks views by missing evidence. At primary budget settings, this pipeline reaches 98.8% closed-loop and 98.9% functional-OOD success; ablating the functional or command-binding components collapses performance to near zero. Seven vision-language models, evaluated on the same cases, reach only 26-43%. Replacing the reference pipeline's coverage metadata with frozen RGB-derived scores preserves 98.5%/98.9% success; shuffling the visual scores collapses it to 15%/11%, confirming that the result depends on image content. These gaps suggest that spatial belief for manipulation should be evaluated not by scene-level completeness, but by whether it acquires the evidence the current command requires.
- 2026-11-06, **SURE:Spatiotemporal Uncertainty Representation for Efficient Vision-Language-Action Learning**, Jingye Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.09684](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09684)
> **Abstract:** Vision-language-action (VLA) policies have become a promising paradigm for end-to-end robot control, but their effectiveness still depends heavily on large volumes of demonstration data. In manipulation tasks, this dependence is compounded by structured spatiotemporal uncertainty in the action space: multiple nearby actions or trajectories may successfully solve the same task, while semantically equivalent demonstrations often unfold at different speeds. Standard pointwise supervision treats each demonstrated action as an exact target, imposing an unnecessary learning burden under such uncertainty. We present SURE, a data-efficient VLA training framework that explicitly models both spatial and temporal uncertainty. SURE augments a VLA backbone with a lightweight transposed-convolutional probabilistic action decoder that captures spatial uncertainty through local correlations among neighboring action bins. It further introduces Soft-DTW alignment supervision to accommodate local timing variations between predicted and demonstrated action chunks. Together, these two components reduce sensitivity to demonstrator-specific poses and execution timing, enabling more data-efficient action learning. We evaluate SURE on LIBERO, RoboTwin 2.0, and real-world manipulation tasks. Even with only 10% of the demonstration data or 20% of the training steps, SURE achieves manipulation success rates that match or surpass baseline VLAs.
- 2026-11-06, **ODEAction: From Predicting Chunks to Evolving Actions**, Qishen Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.09672](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09672)
> **Abstract:** Vision-language-action (VLA) models typically generate actions in chunks, organizing continuous robot execution into separately predicted segments and thus introducing potential discontinuities at action-chunk transitions. We propose ODEAction, a chunk-free action generation paradigm that replaces action-chunk prediction with Continuous Action Evolution. Given visual observations, language instructions, and robot proprioceptive information, ODEAction learns an action transition field defined over action space, where a transition vector characterizes the evolution rate of the current action with respect to evolution time. During execution, the model takes the current action as its initial condition and integrates the learned field using an ordinary differential equation (ODE), recursively generating subsequent actions with explicit dependencies. ODEAction is implemented as an action head and does not depend on a specific vision-language backbone architecture. Experiments on LIBERO, EBench, RoboTwin, and real robots show that ODEAction improves both task performance and trajectory quality over matched action-chunking baselines, with particularly pronounced gains on fine manipulation tasks.
- 2026-11-06, **Decoupled Mode Connectivity for Base-to-Novel Generalization in Vision-Language Models**, Imad Eddine Marouf et.al., Paper: [@2611.09655](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09655)
> **Abstract:** Prompt learning adapts vision-language models such as CLIP by optimizing a short learnable prompt. The cross-entropy objective pulls this prompt toward   base-class specialization, whereas generalization to unseen classes benefits from staying close to CLIP's zero-shot feature space. Because both objectives   act on the same prompt, their gradients conflict at convergence, and in our experiments no single-prompt loss design clearly improves on the cosine   baseline. We propose Decoupled Mode Connectivity (DMC), which gives each objective its own prompt. Starting from a base-trained prompt, DMC trains a   specialization prompt and a generalization prompt, and merges their text features with a fixed coefficient at test time, at the inference cost of a single   prompt. A linear mode connectivity (LMC) corridor in text-feature space keeps the classification loss low for every classifier interpolated between the two   endpoints, and a Visual Anchor KL term preserves CLIP's zero-shot class-similarity distribution of each image during specialization. We further argue that   such regularizers should satisfy class-permutation invariance (CPI), and show via Fano's inequality, in an idealized model, that violating CPI   lower-bounds the drop in novel accuracy by a term proportional to the mutual information between the prompt and the base-class labels. Across 11 datasets,   DMC improves the average harmonic mean of base and novel accuracy over MergeTune for both CoOp and KgCoOp, and its interpolation curves pass beyond the   single-prompt operating point, shifting the base-novel frontier rather than trading along it. Code is available at https://github.com/IemProg/DMC.
- 2026-11-06, **SPSC: Zero-Shot Cross-Subject EEG-to-Image Reconstruction via Semantic Preservation and Subspace Calibration**, Yutang Lu et.al., Paper: [@2611.09380](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09380)
> **Abstract:** Pretrained vision-language models and diffusion generators have driven rapid progress in brain-to-image generation, while EEG-based reconstruction is still constrained by low signal-to-noise ratio and cross-subject semantic shifts. Zero-shot cross-subject reconstruction poses even greater challenges: EEG semantic embeddings learned from source subjects deviate from the manifold of image embeddings of the unseen target subject, particularly when neither target labels nor target image embeddings are available. To address this issue, we introduce the Semantic Preservation and Subspace Calibration (SPSC) framework, which first uses Object Mask Contrastive Regularization (OMCR) with object identity for backend training, and then constructs Subspace Candidate Gate (SCG) to achieve label-free semantic correction for target subjects during inference. Specifically, OMCR regularizes the conditioning space with object-level positive samples to preserve object-level feature neighborhoods within the reconstruction prior and low-level branch, while SCG exploits training image prototypes and batch statistics of unlabeled target EEG to select candidates for each task within subspaces of image prototypes. Under the leave-one-subject-out (LOSO) setting across ten subjects on the THINGS-EEG dataset, SPSC improves the cross-subject reconstruction from 0.552 to 0.713 on CLIP and from 0.612 to 0.745 on Alex.5. These significant gains indicate that the semantically calibrated conditions can better support generation for the target subjects under label-free inference.
- 2026-11-06, **Can LLMs Debug Robots by Editing Their Weights?**, Jusheng Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.09376](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09376)
> **Abstract:** A language model can repair a robot policy's lexical instruction grounding by editing just 65 token-embedding rows. We bring weight-space programming to robot foundation policies: GPT-6 Astra reads weights and uses a CPU workspace and budgeted simulator feedback, without gradient updates or new demonstrations. Three independent sessions on $π_{0.5}$ all improve success on 765 held-out paraphrases; the best raises success from 86.3\% to 92.0\% (48 repairs, four regressions; exact paired $p=1.31\times10^{-10}$). Among the 335 instructions containing edited tokens, success rises from 74.3\% to 87.5\%; the other 430 episodes have zero outcome changes. Matched random-direction edits do not help. Full replacement using the editor's own lexicon explains much of the gain but produces more regressions; its direct difference from the editor is not significant at 0.05. Across 13 further robot sessions, the editor declines 12 candidates; the sole retained edit does not show a significant held-out gain. Separately, benchmark metadata lowers FastWAM success from 70.0\% to 48.6\%, while the evaluated VLA's aggregate rate is unchanged. The evidence supports precise lexical repair, not general acquisition of missing robot capabilities.

- 2026-11-04, **Annotator-Free Object Detection via Foundation Model Collaboration**, Yasiru Ranasinghe et.al., Paper: [@2611.06195](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06195)
> **Abstract:** Object detection research remains constrained by high annotation costs and the closed-set assumptions of supervised models, limiting scalability to novel categories. > **Abstract:** Robotic manipulation often requires remembering information that is no longer visible, such as an object's location before occlusion or the order of a demonstrated action sequence. > **Abstract:** Omni models answer questions about videos by jointly processing sampled frames and audio in one turn, with progress driven largely by scaling model size and omni training data. - 2026-11-04, **RI-Prune: Relation and Information Aware Visual Token Pruning for Multi-Image Vision-Language Models**, Dongjin Lee et.al., Paper: [@2611.06047](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06047)
> **Abstract:** Large vision-language models (LVLMs) have recently been extended to handle multiple input images, but this extension dramatically increases the visual token budget, making visual-token pruning not merely an efficiency option but a practical necessity. - 2026-11-04, **USI-Brain: Enhancing Underwater Spatial Intelligence via Spatial Data-Driven Vision-Language Modeling**, Zhiliang Zhu et.al., Paper: [@2611.06040](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06040)

- 2026-11-04, **MotoVerse: A Benchmark for Predictive Perception of Motorized Two-Wheelers in Dense Unstructured Traffic**, Varun A. Paturkar et.al., Paper: [@2611.06007](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06007)
> **Abstract:** Motorized Two-Wheelers (MTWs) dominate personal transport across much of the Global South, yet remain largely overlooked in predictive perception research for autonomous driving and ADAS. The dataset and evaluation code are publicly available at https://github.com/VarunIIITH/MotoVerse.

## World Models & Generative Video

- 2026-11-06, **Video-Coder: Reinforcing Agentic Coding for Video Generation**, Chaoyang Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.09894](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09894)
> **Abstract:** Recent years have witnessed remarkable progress in video generation in terms of visual quality and temporal consistency. However, generating videos with precise spatial structure, coherent motion dynamics, and controllable scene composition remains challenging. In this paper, we introduce Video-Coder, the first attempt to train a code-driven video generation agent, which autonomously writes and executes code to construct a dynamic 3D scene (Draft Video) that provides structure and motion signals to downstream video generators. To support this paradigm, we design a dedicated data pipeline and curate two high-quality datasets, Video-Coder-SFT-6k and Video-Coder-RL-2k, which contain diverse prompts and ground-truth reference videos. We further develop the Video- Coder Harness, a unified interactive environment integrating code execution, 3D scene rendering, and execution feedback for agent training and inference. We then train Video-Coder with SFT fol- lowed by harness-native agentic reinforcement learning with dual reward feedback, which combines execution-based and video-based rewards. Additionally, we design an annealing training strategy that gradually reduces the execution reward over time, allowing the model to rely more on the video reward in the later training stages. Experiments show that Video-Coder achieves significant gains, improving Minimax-H3 by approximately 7 points on PhyGenBench and 24 points on VBench-2.0. We hope this work can serve as an open foundation for coding agents in video generation, and we fully open-source our data, models, and code
- 2026-11-06, **AR-Edit: Training-Free Autoregressive Video Editing without Inversion**, Hovhannes Margaryan et.al., Paper: [@2611.09795](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09795)
> **Abstract:** Editing a video stream in real time, without access to future frames, is essential for interactive applications. Existing training-free video editing methods, however, assume full offline access to the entire sequence, while streaming approaches either rely on additional training or are limited to specific editing techniques. We show that this limitation can be removed by analyzing the self-attention dynamics of DMD-distilled autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models. We uncover a key asymmetry: queries and keys remain stable and aligned with their source counterparts across denoising timesteps, while values diverge and encode the evolving content. Inherited from the non-distilled bidirectional teacher, this asymmetry is strongest at high noise, where few-step streaming models operate. The resulting mechanism is simple: a single forward pass suffices to capture source identity in the value features, which can be reused throughout generation. Starting from pure Gaussian noise and injecting only these source values reconstructs the input with fidelity matching or surpassing inversion-based methods, effectively eliminating inversion. Based on this insight, we introduce AR-Edit, a training-free, inversion-free method that caches source values once and selectively injects them during denoising. Across multiple AR backbones, AR-Edit enables real-time streaming editing with superior source preservation and tonal fidelity at minimal overhead.
- 2026-11-06, **Encoding Memory in World Models with Test-Time Model-Based Reinforcement Learning**, Maxime Burchi et.al., Paper: [@2611.09625](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09625)
> **Abstract:** Model-based reinforcement learning (MBRL) approaches have demonstrated strong performance across diverse domains in recent years, learning accurate world models from agent trajectories. However, despite this progress, current approaches still struggle to surpass human performance on tasks that require long-term memory and planning. In this work, we address the problem of long-term memory in MBRL by moving beyond the commonly studied paradigm of architecture design. We introduce Test-Time Model-Based Reinforcement Learning (TT-MBRL), using gradient descent at test time to encode the memory of episode observations into world model parameters. This enables the agent to learn a distinct world model for each episode, improving memory capabilities compared to traditional approaches that solely rely on contextual memory. We first establish a proof of concept on a simplified two-dimensional variant of the Memory Maze environment, which we refer to as Memory Maze 2D, showing consistent improvements across different world model architectures. We then demonstrate the effectiveness of TT-MBRL in the 3D domain, achieving new state-of-the-art results on the Memory Maze benchmark. Finally, we show that TT-MBRL maintains strong performance on non-memory benchmarks such as DeepMind Control and Atari 100k.
- 2026-11-06, **Trajectory Forcing: Exploiting Diffusion Trajectories for Autoregressive Long Video Generation**, Bohan Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.09096](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09096)
> **Abstract:** Autoregressive (AR) modeling is arguably the only way to extend a fixed-length video diffusion model to longer or even streaming videos. However, existing AR diffusion methods use only the previously predicted chunk as context for the next, discarding the intermediate predictions that constitute the chunk's generation history. In contrast, conventional AR models such as LLMs condition on the entire history of preceding tokens. TraF operates under two training regimes: (1) with ground-truth videos, where pseudo-trajectories are constructed from Gaussian interpolations and re-predicted by the model, and (2) with a teacher model, where real trajectories are obtained from the student's own denoising rollout. The first regime naturally provides a strong initialization for the second. On the 5-second VBench benchmark, TraF achieves a Total score of 85.15, outperforming Self Forcing (+0.84) and Causal Forcing (+1.11), with Quality and Semantic improving simultaneously. On 30-second generation---6$\times$ the training horizon---TraF achieves the highest overall quality among all compared methods.
- 2026-11-06, **Emo-LiveSync: Real-Time Universal Lip Synchronization with Explicit Emotion Control**, Sangjoon Yu et.al., Paper: [@2611.09074](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09074)
> **Abstract:** Lip synchronization, the task of generating realistic lip movements synchronized with audio signals, is fundamental to applications such as film dubbing, virtual avatars, and digital content creation. > **Abstract:** Recent advances in video diffusion models have achieved remarkable photorealistic RGB synthesis, yet generating videos with a native alpha channel remains an open challenge. For generation, UniRGBA outperforms Wan-Alpha on RGBA text-to-video tasks and matches or surpasses the original pretrained Wan2.1 backbone on RGB-only metrics, demonstrating that native alpha support can be integrated without compromising the underlying RGB prior or the model's architectural versatility.
- 2026-11-06, **MotionCore: A Latent Neural Simulator for Video Generation with Rigid-Body Dynamics**, Zheng Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.08904](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08904)
> **Abstract:** Video generation models can produce highly realistic videos while generating physically inconsistent object motion. > **Abstract:** Reconstructing natural visual experience from cortical population activity is a central challenge in neural decoding, particularly for calcium imaging, where signals are noisy, temporally filtered, and paired neural-video data are limited. - 2026-11-05, **InstaGen: Group-Shared Importance Sampling for Fast Video Generation**, Kislay Aditya Oj et.al., Paper: [@2611.08610](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08610)
> **Abstract:** Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) are the dominant architecture for video generation, but their quadratic attention complexity results in substantial computational cost and latency. > **Abstract:** Modern video models possess strong visual priors, yet do not natively represent human action in metric 3D space. - 2026-11-05, **ManimCode: Learning Visual Self-Correction for Educational Animation**, Zhengyang Liang et.al., Paper: [@2611.08294](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08294)
> **Abstract:** Manim supports precise, programmatic video generation, but a successfully rendered educational animation can still have visual defects, missing explanations, or incorrect content. > **Abstract:** World-action models adapt pretrained video generators, and vision-language-action models build on pretrained vision-language backbones; both tie policy architecture and inference cost to large transformers designed for generation rather than control. > **Abstract:** Recent advances in video generation have achieved impressive visual quality, yet they often fail to produce physically consistent dynamics due to the lack of explicit modeling of underlying physical processes. > **Abstract:** Planning with world models enables test-time adaptation for embodied agents, offering superior generalization over direct policy learning. > **Abstract:** Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD) trains a few-step student using the difference between a teacher's score and an online critic's estimate of the student distribution's score. > **Abstract:** Few-step flow models generate samples through a handful of large denoising transitions. > **Abstract:** Continuous-time world models generate a latent trajectory through a single ODE integration, but adding stochastic conditioning does not ensure that the model uses it. > **Abstract:** Robot perception is increasingly data driven, yet field data is slow, costly, and sometimes disruptive to collect. ## Computational Design, Mechanism & Fabrication

- 2026-11-06, **Segmenting Geometric Textures of 3D Meshes**, Weidan Xiong et.al., Paper: [@2611.08897](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08897)
> **Abstract:** Recognizing geometric texture patterns on 3D models is critical for applications such as heritage protection, model editing, and 3D printing. Existing 3D model segmentation methods predominantly focus on identifying high-level semantic parts, thereby failing to capture middle-level textures. While recent Vision-Language Models can perform image segmentation with appropriate text prompts, they still struggle to recognize 3D geometric textures. In this work, we present a method that segments 3D mesh models based on middle-level geometric textures. We further introduce a new geometric texture segmentation benchmark and fine-tune SAM3 accordingly. The segmented texture regions are then clustered based on DINO feature similarity and lifted from 2D to 3D. Experiments demonstrate that our method significantly outperforms existing methods in segmenting geometric textures and can facilitate various applications.
- 2026-11-02, **Learning Purposeful Co-designs of Bio-inspired Quadrupedal Robots**, Fabio Boekel et.al., Paper: [@2611.02462](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02462)
> **Abstract:** Treating morphology and control as separate design problems has constrained the efficiency and adaptability of quadrupedal robots, while current co-design approaches are often constrained by restricted design spaces. A two-stage framework combining reinforcement learning and Bayesian optimization is developed: the morphology-conditioned control policy is trained across designs and then held fixed during task-specific design selection. The resulting designs and policies deployed zero-shot reduce the cost of transport by 18.6% and 35.7% for inspection and payload-carrying tasks. - 2026-10-30, **Toolpath Overhang Optimization for the Large-Format 3D Printing of Bespoke Shapes**, Yefan Zhi et.al., Paper: [@2611.00371](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00371)
> **Abstract:** This paper presents a closed curve trimming algorithm for overhang optimization in large-format support-free 3D printing of bespoke manifold shapes. Because of the material properties and surface quality requirements, such shapes are typically printed without support structures and have to be designed as support-free. Most current methods of overhang optimization are limited to certain types of geometry and do not apply to bespoke shapes. Our method directly optimizes sliced toolpath curves with minimal geometric modification. Given an overhang limit, it ensures that all resulting toolpath curves are strictly within the overhang limit while preserving their orientation and preventing intersections. - 2026-10-29, **SimTO-Robust: Automated Design of Soft Grippers Robust to Grasp Pose and Object Geometry**, Kurt Enkera et.al., Paper: [@2610.39560](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39560)
> **Abstract:** Robotic grippers operating in unstructured environments must robustly grasp objects of different shapes and sizes despite perception and positioning errors. ## Sketch-based & Creative HCI Tools

- 2026-11-03, **OmniScribble: Mixed-Initiative Authoring of Animated Sketches through Inspectable and Repairable AI Decisions**, Hien Trinh et.al., Paper: [@2611.04284](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04284)
> **Abstract:** AI-assisted creative tools reduce the effort of authoring content, but delegating creative decisions to AI can make it difficult for authors to understand, correct, or redirect work. We present OmniScribble, a system for animating multi-object sketches that treats object decomposition and motion planning as editable artifacts. Rather than generating animation end-to-end, OmniScribble presents AI proposals at verification checkpoints, where authors can accept, repair, or regenerate them. Both manual and AI-assisted authoring operate on the intermediate representation and rendering pipeline, allowing us to isolate AI delegation. In a within-subject study with eight participants, AI-assisted authoring yielded a median within-participant reduction in completion time of 71.4\%, while perceived control and motion-intent alignment remained high. Interaction traces and interviews show that participants adapted to AI errors by repairing local issues, regenerating mismatches, and accepting proposals. These findings suggest that inspectable, repairable delegation enables generative automation while maintaining control over the outcome.
- 2026-11-01, **Capable but Not Scalable: Requirements and Interaction Costs of Building Multi-View CAD Datasets for Design Research**, Danyal Sarfraz et.al., Paper: [@2611.02108](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02108)
> **Abstract:** Transforming CAD models into consistent multi-view image datasets is increasingly important for AI-driven product design research, yet existing workflows can impose substantial burdens on researchers without programming expertise. We examine this challenge from a human-computer interaction (HCI) perspective by analyzing ten reported rendering practices and deriving fourteen requirements spanning dataset quality, visual consistency, and user interaction. We then assess eleven development- and design-oriented tools against an illustrative task involving STEP models, interpreting potential interaction costs through the cognitive dimensions framework. Our analysis reveals a gap between technical capability and practical scalability: programming-based frameworks offer flexible automation but require coding expertise, while graphical design tools provide accessible functionality yet retain repetitive per-product operations. No assessed workflow combines a low programming threshold, low repetition viscosity, and flexible protocol configuration. Based on these findings, we propose design directions including reusable rendering presets, collection-wide previews and corrections, mixed-initiative orientation assignment, and automatic provenance recording. This work reframes CAD dataset generation as an end-user interaction design problem and establishes requirements for more accessible, scalable dataset creation tools.
- 2026-10-28, **StreamStudio: Exploring Video Authoring with Streaming Autoregressive Generation**, Boyu Li et.al., Paper: [@2610.38467](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38467)
> **Abstract:** We present StreamStudio, a prototype system that explores a new authoring paradigm for streaming video generation. Instead of entering a prompt and waiting for a complete video before making changes, creators can interact with the generation as it unfolds: steering what comes next, returning to earlier moments, and exploring different continuations. In this way, StreamStudio shifts creative control from post-generation revision to continuous interaction with the generative process. Our contribution centers on this authoring paradigm and the interaction mechanisms that make streaming generation directly manipulable. StreamStudio is designed as a plug-and-play interface that can work with compatible generation and editing models, allowing its interaction model to evolve alongside advances in underlying video models. As streaming models become faster and easier to control in the future, such an interface may support more flexible and controllable video creation, including richer edits and multimodal interventions during generation.
- 2026-10-28, **LogicTree: Object-Oriented Writing for Structured Human-AI Academic Authoring**, Jiajun Zhu et.al., Paper: [@2610.38136](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38136)
> **Abstract:** Academic writing is non-linear, iterative, and structure-driven, requiring authors to develop fragmented ideas into arguments with local coherence and global consistency. - 2026-10-26, **The CAPED Framework: Characterizing User Skills in Chart Authoring through the Lens of Tasks and Tools**, Bongshin Lee et.al., Paper: [@2610.34652](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34652)
> **Abstract:** In this paper, we introduce a new framework for characterizing the user skills required for chart authoring. ## 3D Understanding: Reconstruction & Generation

- 2026-11-06, **OmniLatent: Unified Latent for 3D Part Modeling**, Yunhan Yang et.al., Paper: [@2611.09055](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09055)
> **Abstract:** 3D part decomposition is a central capability for editable asset creation, yet existing workflows typically segment a completed mesh as a post-processing step, making the result sensitive to geometric artifacts, texture ambiguity, and limited part-level annotations. We present OmniLatent, a unified latent modeling framework for 3D part decomposition. Instead of predicting part labels in isolation, OmniLatent learns a shared latent space that jointly encodes geometry, deformation, appearance, and binary part IDs, allowing data-rich geometric and visual priors to support data-scarce part learning. Given a single input image, the base model generates geometry, deformation, texture, and surface part IDs in one pass, producing a part-aware 3D asset without a separate segmentation stage. The key idea is to leverage a complexity prior computed via the local entropy in geometric and textural characteristics of a clothed human. This effectively prevents Gaussians from being coarsely placed in complex regions, which facilitates the faithful reconstruction of intricate structures. Furthermore, we sample local patches predominantly from complex regions according to this complexity prior, which allows the model to be sufficiently supervised by local details. Experimental results on benchmark datasets demonstrate that our method delivers state-of-the-art performance while maintaining the real-time inference speed in generalizable human Gaussian splatting. The code and model are publicly available at https://github.com/DCVL-3D/CGR-GS_release.
- 2026-10-26, **DUET4D: Self-Corrective Coupled Guidance for Sparse-View 4D Reconstruction**, Beizhen Zhao et.al., Paper: [@2610.35002](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35002)
> **Abstract:** Sparse-view 4D reconstruction is highly under-constrained when only a few cameras are available. Generative priors can recover missing appearances, but video models are computationally impractical for iterative reconstruction, whereas single-view models produce temporally inconsistent pseudo-observations. Moreover, directly absorbing uncertain generative outputs into Gaussian optimization often creates redundant primitives and floating artifacts. To address these challenges, we propose DUET4D, a self-corrective coupled guidance framework for sparse-view 4D Gaussian splatting. The core of our approach is a self-corrective framework where the 4D representation guides generative prior for spatio-temporal consistent supervisions. We first construct sequential alignments from current Gaussian evidence to steer the latent restoration. Then we use Sequential Fidelity Resampling to lift generative supervisions into Gaussian space to selectively distinguish repeatedly supported primitives. The corrected representation then provides more reliable Gaussian evidence for the next restoration cycle. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DUET4D significantly improves rendering quality and rendering efficiency.
- 2026-10-26, **Recovering Black-Box Point Cloud Models from Pose Shifts via Derivative-Free Optimization**, Seonghwan Park et.al., Paper: [@2610.34850](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34850)
> **Abstract:** Point cloud models are increasingly deployed through score-only black-box interfaces. Under this access model, test-time rotations outside the training distribution can substantially degrade performance. Although existing mitigations such as equivariant retraining and gradient-based adaptation are effective, they are infeasible without access to model weights or gradients. To address this limitation, we propose CanDO, a test-time canonicalization method that recovers lost performance using only forward queries. Specifically, CanDO searches for an input transformation that maximizes model confidence and uses derivative-free optimization to perform this search without gradients. We further identify three conditions for successful recovery: a training augmentation gap, an accuracy-aligned confidence surrogate, and global search over a multi-modal objective. Across multiple architectures and benchmarks, CanDO recovers 76% of the oracle gap under azimuthal misorientation and 51% under arbitrary SO(3) rotations for object part segmentation. These results show that frozen score-only point cloud models can recover from rotation-induced distribution shifts through post-deployment canonicalization alone.
- 2026-10-26, **PosePlaner: Denoising Any Feed-Forward Pose Predictor from Pairwise Planar Geometry**, Yihang Chen et.al., Paper: [@2610.34845](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34845)
> **Abstract:** Recent multi-view Transformers (e.g., DUSt3R and VGGT) have advanced 3D reconstruction by scaling training data and model capacity from two views to many views and long sequences, subsuming the classical SfM/SLAM pipeline of feature extraction, matching, and pose estimation in a single feed-forward pass. Yet this subsumption hides the interfaces between pipeline stages---correspondences, geometry, and pose are entangled in a single forward pass, leaving supervised fine-tuning as the major path to improvement and offering no mechanism to correct predictions from geometric evidence at test time. We propose PosePlaner to close this gap by reviving the classical coupling between correspondences and pose via flow matching, treating pose refinement as a conditional generation problem that takes coarse geometric matches and initial pose as input condition and fuses the resulting pose hypotheses into a single refined estimate, providing a continuous, data-driven analog of RANSAC voting. Our method is trained efficiently on two-view data, yet serves as a periodic refiner for streaming n-view pose estimators with no retraining. Empirically, it corrects failures of feed-forward predictors, substantially improves correspondence-based solvers, and reduces accumulated drift in long streaming sequences.
- 2026-10-26, **SceneAgent: An Agentic Pipeline from 3D and 4D Captures to Simulation Environments**, Luke Hollis et.al., Paper: [@2610.34745](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34745)
> **Abstract:** Visual captures provide detailed scene appearance, but converting them into interactive simulation environments requires object reconstruction, physical modeling, and correction of intermediate errors. We present SceneAgent, an agentic pipeline that coordinates these steps through simulator-based visual inspection and repair. The pipeline processes 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), photogrammetry, LiDAR captures, AI-generated 3DGS scenes, and 4D videos of dynamic scenes, and combines object segmentation, physical parameter estimation, part decomposition, articulation, and deformable simulation. To support policy learning, SceneAgent generates diverse object variants ("digital sisters") and scripted demonstrations. On a cluttered bin scene from GraspClutter6D, SceneAgent raises F1 at 1 cm for every coding agent with scorable outputs both with and without its skills, and its reconstructions are better than SimFoundry: all agent configurations exceed SimFoundry's F1 score, with a best of 60.75% versus 6.9%. ## Embodied AI & Robot Learning

- 2026-11-06, **Encoding Memory in World Models with Test-Time Model-Based Reinforcement Learning**, Maxime Burchi et.al., Paper: [@2611.09625](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09625)
> **Abstract:** Model-based reinforcement learning (MBRL) approaches have demonstrated strong performance across diverse domains in recent years, learning accurate world models from agent trajectories. However, despite this progress, current approaches still struggle to surpass human performance on tasks that require long-term memory and planning. In this work, we address the problem of long-term memory in MBRL by moving beyond the commonly studied paradigm of architecture design. We introduce Test-Time Model-Based Reinforcement Learning (TT-MBRL), using gradient descent at test time to encode the memory of episode observations into world model parameters. This enables the agent to learn a distinct world model for each episode, improving memory capabilities compared to traditional approaches that solely rely on contextual memory. We first establish a proof of concept on a simplified two-dimensional variant of the Memory Maze environment, which we refer to as Memory Maze 2D, showing consistent improvements across different world model architectures. We then demonstrate the effectiveness of TT-MBRL in the 3D domain, achieving new state-of-the-art results on the Memory Maze benchmark. Finally, we show that TT-MBRL maintains strong performance on non-memory benchmarks such as DeepMind Control and Atari 100k.
- 2026-11-06, **Probably Safe Sim-to-Real Transfer with Lifetime Validity**, Rohan Tangri et.al., Paper: [@2611.09344](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09344)
> **Abstract:** We present Dejavu, a method that makes sim-to-real transfer of reinforcement learning policies in constrained Markov decision processes probably safe over an unbounded sequence of policy updates. An anytime valid test using per-decision importance sampling and a betting confidence sequence certifies each candidate trained in simulation before deployment using only trajectories collected by a safe baseline policy operating in production. Simulation quality does not affect the safety guarantee, only the production sample efficiency of the method. Finally, a summable per-test failure probability yields the lifetime valid property. Empirical results across three sim-to-real benchmarks support the theoretical work.
- 2026-11-06, **SUGARv2: Learning Diverse Visual Humanoid Loco-Manipulation Skills from Synthesized Interaction References**, Tianshu Wu et.al., Paper: [@2611.09266](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09266)
> **Abstract:** Scaling up visual humanoid loco-manipulation skill acquisition requires reducing the human effort needed as tasks and spatial configurations expand, particularly in demonstration collection and task-specific training design. We present SUGARv2, a unified framework for learning diverse visual humanoid loco-manipulation skills through interaction reference synthesis and a shared training pipeline. To obtain diverse references at low cost, we adapt reusable task templates to different spatial configurations, synthesizing coordinated robot and object motions. To reduce task-specific reward design and training effort, we use reference-based imitation as a common learning objective across tasks. We first train privileged teachers to execute these references, then train visual policies through teacher distillation and reinforcement learning with the same reference-based mimic rewards, using diverse object instances and visual domain randomization to support visual generalization and sim-to-real transfer. Across five humanoid loco-manipulation tasks, SUGARv2 achieves a mean success rate of 91.0% in simulation. Deployment on a real humanoid robot further demonstrates successful sim-to-real transfer for whole-body manipulation. These results demonstrate the framework's potential for learning diverse visual humanoid loco-manipulation skills.
- 2026-11-06, **ToolDex: An Ego-Exo Human Demonstration Dataset for Creative Physical Tool Use**, Mohamed Elmoghany et.al., Paper: [@2611.09036](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09036)
> **Abstract:** Creative tool use is a fundamental human capability and an important step toward general-purpose embodied intelligence. It requires agents to reason about object affordances, physical constraints, and manipulation strategies, especially when standard tools are unavailable. However, progress in this area is limited by the lack of real-world datasets that capture human tool-use behavior with sufficient scale, fidelity, and diversity. Existing benchmarks are often simulation-based, focus on simplified interactions, or lack detailed observations and annotations.   We introduce ToolDex, a large-scale egocentric-exocentric (ego-exo) human demonstration dataset for creative physical tool use. ToolDex captures scenarios in which humans select, adapt, and repurpose available objects as tools. Using synchronized egocentric cameras and eight exocentric cameras, the dataset provides high-fidelity multi-view observations and rich annotations for hands, tools, target objects, and their deformable interactions. Beyond data collection, we establish a benchmark suite characterizing three core capabilities: physical tool-use reasoning, sparse-view 3D and 4D reconstruction, and robot policy learning. ToolDex provides a foundation for studying human tool-use behavior and developing embodied agents that can adaptively use tools in complex real-world environments.   Dataset visualizations and benchmarking results are available at https://tooluse-dataset.github.io/.
- 2026-11-06, **RevoSim: Scalable Multimodal Tactile Simulation for Dexterous Manipulation**, Ke Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.08939](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08939)
> **Abstract:** We present RevoSim, a scalable and high-fidelity multimodal tactile simulator for the BrainCo Revo3 hand that jointly models 285 piezoresistive taxels and five visuotactile fingertips. RevoSim efficiently generates synchronized pressure, depth, RGB, and marker-displacement observations; on a single RTX 5090, its depth-conditioned marker-displacement configuration supports up to 4,096 parallel environments. We train in-hand manipulation and grasping policies entirely in simulation, establishing a foundation for future sim-to-real transfer. Project page: https://braincotech.github.io/RevoSim-web/.
- 2026-11-06, **STRIDE: Learning Humanoid Locomotion in Minutes with Acceleration-Based Rewards**, Nico Bohlinger et.al., Paper: [@2611.08857](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08857)
> **Abstract:** Learning locomotion directly on a humanoid robot is a promising direction to avoid the sim-to-real gap. However, locomotion rewards in Reinforcement Learning (RL) typically track the base velocity, which cannot be measured onboard without an external motion capture system or well-calibrated state estimator. This is why we introduce \method{}, which learns humanoid locomotion from minutes of samples with a reward that only requires the onboard Inertial Measurement Unit (IMU). Instead of tracking the base velocity, STRIDE rewards the robot for following a commanded acceleration profile. To learn efficiently from this reward, we use the off-policy RL algorithm XQC and combine a high gain setting for the joint position controllers with left-right symmetry augmentation. In simulation, STRIDE learns to walk on the Unitree G1 and Booster T1 humanoids within 14 minutes of samples. Compared to learning without these two components, it reduces the falls during training by 81%, while without them the robot learns only in over an hour.
- 2026-11-05, **Latent RL Priors for Data-Efficient Dexterous Manipulation**, Yixiao Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.08458](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08458)
> **Abstract:** Learning dexterous manipulation from limited visual demonstrations is challenging because policies must acquire both perceptual grounding and precise control, while reinforcement learning (RL) in simulation can acquire rich control behaviors from large-scale interaction with privileged information. We introduce \textbf{Latent RL Prior}, a two-stage framework that reuses this control knowledge for data-efficient visual policy learning. During privileged pretraining, we distill large-scale RL rollouts into a flow-matching action decoder conditioned on proprioception and latent task context. During visual adaptation, we freeze the decoder and learn a visual interface that maps images to its latent context using action supervision alone, without privileged labels or latent targets. The same decoder can further be shared across arms by learning coordinated visual contexts for bimanual manipulation. Across three simulated tasks, our method outperforms full-parameter fine-tuning of $π_{0.5}$ and GR00T N1.7 in the low-data regime; on door lifting and bimanual assembly, it surpasses full-data baselines using $10\times$ and $5\times$ fewer demonstrations, respectively. Further analysis shows that direct visual-to-latent conditioning improves adaptation performance and training stability over routing visual predictions through the pretrained state encoder. Robustness to unseen mass variation and greater linear accessibility of contact-force information in decoder representations further provide evidence of retained RL-acquired control knowledge. Project website: https://yixiaowang7.github.io/latent-rl-prior-page/.
- 2026-11-05, **4D Point Splatting for mmWave Radar Micro-Doppler Differentiable Rendering**, Adnan Armouti et.al., Paper: [@2611.08342](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08342)
> **Abstract:** Millimeter-wave radar resolves the micrometer displacements of a moving body as micro-Doppler, enabling gait, gesture, activity, pose and vital-sign sensing. Each task relies on a downstream model trained on real data, yet radar datasets lag orders of magnitude behind vision and language. Brute-force collection will not close that gap, and existing synthesis methods (forward-only simulators, learned generators and static-scene inverse renderers) cannot fit a real capture of a moving body, leaving a sim2real gap. We present 4D Point Splatting (4DPS), a physics-based differentiable renderer that fits an explicit moving scene to a real capture. Using the synchronized camera to initialize the room and body, we represent the scene as oriented points carrying material, normal and velocity, and splat each point into range and Doppler bins. Our output is the raw complex return a real radar records, so we apply the same signal processing on the rendered data to obtain any representation for training any SOTA downstream model. We fit 235 sequences on HuPR and 100 on RT-Pose, two datasets with different radars. Our reconstructions set the state of the art, with median correlations of 0.940 and 0.830 on range-azimuth, 0.829 and 0.695 on range-Doppler, and 0.988 and 0.966 on micro-Doppler. Our renders also set the state of the art in downstream sim2real gap. On RT-Pose, a pose model trained on them reaches 21.9 cm MPJPE on real data, against 16.7 cm for real training and 127.6 cm for the RF-Genesis baseline, where both the rendered- and real-trained models learn nearly the same features. Our custom CUDA kernels fit a sequence in 5.7 minutes on HuPR and 12.9 on RT-Pose on one H100, making dataset-scale inverse rendering of mmWave radar possible for the first time. This work can enable synthetic data generation for any human-centered downstream task and model, and scene editing for data augmentation.
- 2026-11-05, **AffordJEV: Affordance Grounding by Confirming Candidate Regions**, Jincheng Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.07896](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07896)
> **Abstract:** Affordance grounding asks which visible part of an object performs a requested operation, a basic capability for embodied manipulation and human--robot interaction. Most existing methods follow a System Two paradigm: a multimodal language model first writes a chain of thought and then turns its conclusion into a box or points for a segmenter. This incurs considerable inference cost, gives the semantic decision little direct control over the extent of the final region, and makes mistakes hard to correct. Yet locating an affordance is essentially a perceptual judgment that need not be argued out in words. We therefore propose, in which frozen segmenters propose candidate regions and a System One model decides directly which of them perform the operation, generating no text and reading each decision from a single forward pass. Our contributions are twofold. (1) An inference framework that confirms candidate regions level by level, from object to part to sub-part, with evidence passed between the levels in both directions so that the levels correct one another. (2) A System One model trained for this framework: existing System One models are built for general-purpose decisions, judge one candidate per request and are ill-suited to the framework's region questions, so ours learns directly from the requests the framework itself produces and judges all candidates of a level at once. Trained only on InstructPart and evaluated with definition-aligned operation words, outperforms chain-of-thought methods in domain and in zero-shot transfer to UMD and IIT-AFF, is better overall than existing System One models run in the same framework, and needs fewer judge requests. To our knowledge, is the first approach to bring System One models to affordance grounding.
- 2026-11-05, **TactileLab: Sim-to-Real Dexterous Robot Manipulation with Efficient Shear-Sensitive Tactile Simulation**, Yijiong Lin et.al., Paper: [@2611.07881](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07881)
> **Abstract:** Robotic simulators that represent shear within tactile sensing are becoming increasingly important for contact-rich manipulation. Here we introduce \textit{TactileLab}, a scalable multimodal tactile simulation framework built on IsaacLab, with a particular focus on a new optical-flow-based tactile representation termed \textit{simulated tactile flow}. Instead of relying on soft-body physics to model shear deformation, TactileLab uses rigid-body simulation to efficiently estimate dense tangential motion at the contact interface, producing tactile flow fields that encode shear-relevant contact dynamics. This flow field complements geometric features from a contact depth field. We further introduce a novel policy architecture for jointly processing long-horizon proprioceptive signals and rich tactile observations. Across multiple contact-rich tasks, policies trained using simulated tactile flow learn efficiently at scale and exhibit emergent contact-aware behaviours. To facilitate sim-to-real policy deployment, we then propose a novel real-to-sim tactile translation method that maps real tactile image streams to simulated tactile flow and depth fields, together with a new tactile data collection method, and validate it across diverse tactile sensor designs. - 2026-11-05, **MessyNav: Zero-Shot Navigation via Manipulation in Messy Environments**, Kenneth Llontop et.al., Paper: [@2611.07417](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07417)
> **Abstract:** Real-world environments are messy, with paths often blocked by everyday objects like chairs and toys, requiring robots to perform interactive navigation by manipulating obstacles to clear a path. - 2026-11-05, **An Empirical Study of Reinforcement Learning for Keypoint Positioning of Deformable Linear Objects**, Vinay Rodrigues et.al., Paper: [@2611.07261](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07261)
> **Abstract:** Deformable linear object (DLO) manipulation is challenging because high-dimensional configurations, nonlinear contact dynamics, and diverse material and geometric properties produce a broad range of object responses that are both difficult to model and data-intensive to learn. - 2026-11-04, **Transforming Integer Grids to Spatial Intuition: Perception, Not Planning, Governs ARC-AGI-3**, Harsidak Singh Banwait et.al., Paper: [@2611.06793](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06793)
> **Abstract:** Sample-efficient fluid intelligence requires agents that rapidly form working models of unfamiliar interactive environments. - 2026-11-04, **Vortex-Induced Torque in Grasping Soft Objects: Influence of Geometry and Pneumatic Parameters**, Roman Mykhailyshyn et.al., Paper: [@2611.06359](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06359)
> **Abstract:** Vortex grippers are commonly integrated into automated systems for handling objects with complex geometries in packaging, sorting, and assembling. - 2026-11-03, **GRASP: Gradient-Guided Sampling for Contact-Rich Physics-Based Motion Retargeting**, Yang Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.05754](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05754)
> **Abstract:** We study physics-based retargeting of human demonstrations for robot learning: finding robot controls that reproduce reference motions under contact dynamics. > **Abstract:** Dexterous manipulation involves not only moving objects, but also arranging the hand around them to maintain useful contacts and enable subsequent actions. - 2026-11-02, **FEIJOA: Task-Driven Grounding and Execution for Mobile Manipulation**, Aditya Agarwal et.al., Paper: [@2611.04080](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04080)
> **Abstract:** A mobile manipulator asked to "put the toys away in the toy box" must decide which objects are toys and which container the user means, then find base poses, grasps, and placements it can execute. > **Abstract:** The uncertainty of a statistical model is most commonly factorized into an aleatoric and an epistemic part. > **Abstract:** We describe our approach to the V2X-Real track of the UrbanTwin Sim2Real LiDAR Challenge, in which a 3D object detector must be trained exclusively on synthetic data and evaluated on unseen real roadside LiDAR from an Ouster OS1-64 unit, while the synthetic point clouds are simultaneously scored for distributional realism.
