Negative results are reported at equal length, and Section 9 gives a full account of data provenance and compliance. > **Abstract:** Recently, LLM-based autonomous agents have shown strong capabilities in iteratively generating, evaluating, and refining executable solutions. - 2026-10-26, **Provably Efficient Q-learning for Robust Reinforcement Learning**, Yuchen Jiao et.al., Paper: [@2610.35656](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35656)
> **Abstract:** Standard reinforcement learning (RL) algorithms based on Markov Decision Processes (MDPs) are vulnerable to sim-to-real gaps. To address this issue, distributionally robust RL algorithms based on robust MDPs (RMDPs) have been proposed, for ensuring that agents can perform reliably to unpredictable conditions in real-world environments. In this work, we take a step towards settling this problem by proposing a sample efficient (asynchronous Q-learning) algorithm for robust RL. In particular, consider a $γ$-discounted infinite-horizon RMDP with state space $\mathcal{S}$ and action space $\mathcal{A}$, where the uncertainty set is specified by the total variation (TV) distance with uncertainty level $σ$. Assuming access to a single trajectory of Markovian samples induced by a behavior policy and the nominal transition kernel, the sample complexity of the proposed Q-learning algorithm --- to achieve an entrywise $\varepsilon$-approximation of the optimal Q-function --- is bounded by $$ \frac{1}{μ_{\mathsf{min}}\varepsilon^2(1-γ)^3\max\{1-γ,σ\}} + \frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{μ_{\mathsf{min}}(1-γ)} $$ up to logarithmic factors. Here, $t_{\mathsf{mix}}$ and $μ_{\mathsf{min}}$ denote the mixing time and the minimum state-action occupancy probability of the sample trajectory, respectively. > **Abstract:** Existing manipulation data collection methods typically offer either accurate robot-state acquisition through kinematic-chain-based teleoperation or convenient, portable operation through vision-based approaches. - 2026-10-26, **The Language of Deformation: Semantic Failure Analysis as a Prerequisite for Dexterous Manipulation of Deformable Objects**, Ignacio Cuiral-Zueco et.al., Paper: [@2610.35103](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35103)
> **Abstract:** Dexterous manipulation of rigid objects has reached a level of maturity where grasp stability, control, and planning strategies are successfully applied in industry and logistics. ## Neural Rendering & Physics Simulation

- 2026-11-05, **Dynamic GPU Voxel Grids and Voxel-sequential Processing in NanoVDB**, Eftychios Sifakis et.al., Paper: [@2611.08665](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08665)
> **Abstract:** We introduce two fundamental advances to the NanoVDB data structure: dynamic GPU-native topological updates, and a voxel-sequential access paradigm that dramatically accelerates point-wise and stencil-based operators on sparse grids. NanoVDB's linearized memory layout has delivered strong GPU performance, and the Index-Grid concept introduced in the fVDB framework [Williams et al 2024] provided topology/data separation and optimal compactness of storage in sparse machine learning workloads. However, all these prior iterations of NanoVDB assume a static topology that has precluded use of this data structure in simulation and dynamic geometric processes. We eliminate this limitation by introducing a suite of dynamic topology operators -- morphological dilation, grid merging, pruning, and cross-resolution coarsening and refinement. These operators use bit-parallel algorithms to reconstruct grids containing hundreds of millions of voxels in just a few milliseconds.   We also introduce a voxel-sequential access paradigm and data structure for GPU kernels operating on sparse data. We stream active voxels as a flat linear sequence, minimizing or eliminating the need for tree traversal in the process. This achieves compute density and regularity that allow even complex stencil-based operators to approach peak memory bandwidth, with minimal storage overhead. Together, these contributions remove the read-only constraint from NanoVDB, establishing it as a high-performance primary representation for dynamic GPU simulation and geometry manipulation. We evaluate the efficacy of our newly introduced operators in benchmarks sourced from applications such as 3D reconstruction, fluid simulation, and large-scale mesh voxelization.
- 2026-11-05, **4D Point Splatting for mmWave Radar Micro-Doppler Differentiable Rendering**, Adnan Armouti et.al., Paper: [@2611.08342](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08342)
> **Abstract:** Millimeter-wave radar resolves the micrometer displacements of a moving body as micro-Doppler, enabling gait, gesture, activity, pose and vital-sign sensing. Each task relies on a downstream model trained on real data, yet radar datasets lag orders of magnitude behind vision and language. Brute-force collection will not close that gap, and existing synthesis methods (forward-only simulators, learned generators and static-scene inverse renderers) cannot fit a real capture of a moving body, leaving a sim2real gap. We present 4D Point Splatting (4DPS), a physics-based differentiable renderer that fits an explicit moving scene to a real capture. Using the synchronized camera to initialize the room and body, we represent the scene as oriented points carrying material, normal and velocity, and splat each point into range and Doppler bins. Our output is the raw complex return a real radar records, so we apply the same signal processing on the rendered data to obtain any representation for training any SOTA downstream model. We fit 235 sequences on HuPR and 100 on RT-Pose, two datasets with different radars. Our reconstructions set the state of the art, with median correlations of 0.940 and 0.830 on range-azimuth, 0.829 and 0.695 on range-Doppler, and 0.988 and 0.966 on micro-Doppler. Our renders also set the state of the art in downstream sim2real gap. On RT-Pose, a pose model trained on them reaches 21.9 cm MPJPE on real data, against 16.7 cm for real training and 127.6 cm for the RF-Genesis baseline, where both the rendered- and real-trained models learn nearly the same features. Our custom CUDA kernels fit a sequence in 5.7 minutes on HuPR and 12.9 on RT-Pose on one H100, making dataset-scale inverse rendering of mmWave radar possible for the first time. This work can enable synthetic data generation for any human-centered downstream task and model, and scene editing for data augmentation.
- 2026-11-05, **PlayGen2: Learning Real-Time Playable Worlds with Explicit 3D State**, Jihai Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.07867](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07867)
> **Abstract:** Building a playable world requires more than generating plausible video: it demands responsive control, persistent spatial structure, and coherent interactions with dynamic entities. Within a fixed-size local region, its world-state model jointly learns action-conditioned entity dynamics, geometry completion, and entity discovery, committing updates to a persistent world memory. A separate neural renderer synthesizes first-person observations from depth and semantic projections of the world state, without feeding generated images back into state prediction. Using Doom as a testbed, we demonstrate that the model successfully learns world dynamics including geometry-aware player motion and player--NPC interactions. The framework is computationally efficient, enabling the complete simulation and rendering pipeline to achieve approximately 160ms backend latency on a MacBook Pro with an M4 Pro chip. Beyond single-player interaction, we demonstrate zero-shot deployment on unseen Doom maps, multiplayer interaction through the composition of single-player models, and programmable gameplay through rule-based state interventions. These capabilities demonstrate the transferability and extensibility of the learned game-world model. Code and pretrained model weights are publicly available.
- 2026-11-05, **Integrating Learned Priors through Prior-Space Reparameterization for Physically Based Inverse Rendering**, Hun Hwangbo et.al., Paper: [@2611.07573](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07573)
> **Abstract:** Physically based inverse rendering decomposes appearance into materials and illumination, an ambiguous problem for which learned material predictions provide useful prior information. Most existing methods incorporate these cues through auxiliary regularization losses, which alter the optimization objective and require balancing their influence against photometric fitting. We propose a prior-guided material reparameterization that uses the predicted feature relationships to restrict the optimization space while leaving the objective unchanged. We fuse multi-view material predictions onto a mesh and construct a permutohedral lattice in the resulting prior feature space. Surface points with similar predictions share material variables, which are optimized jointly with geometry and illumination using a purely photometric objective. Experiments with two learned predictors on three synthetic benchmarks and Stanford-ORB demonstrate improved material decomposition and relighting. Our method improves relighting PSNR by 1.5-2.5 dB over the strongest evaluated mesh-based baseline on Synthetic4Relight and TensoIR-Synthetic, and outperforms a regularization loss using the same prior in controlled comparisons. We further show that our reparameterization pools noisy photometric gradients, producing updates that are more consistent across sampled views. This consistency enables a shortened joint optimization schedule that takes less than a minute while retaining relighting quality above all evaluated baselines on TensoIR-Synthetic.
- 2026-11-02, **IMUdrape: Generating Garment-Mounted Virtual IMU Data from Text through Physics-Based Cloth Simulation**, Zikang Leng et.al., Paper: [@2611.02393](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02393)
> **Abstract:** Inertial sensors are moving into clothing, from sensorized sportswear to garments instrumented with hundreds of IMUs. Sewn into clothing, a sensor no longer moves rigidly with the body: loose fabric slides, twists and swings, so a garment-mounted sensor records the motion of the fabric as well as that of the body, and this extra motion differs from one garment to the next. Models for such sensors need training data recorded with the deployed garment, yet labeled data are scarce. Virtual IMU data generated from videos or text have eased this scarcity for body-attached sensors, but existing generators attach their virtual sensors rigidly to the body. We present IMUdrape, which generates garment-mounted virtual IMU data from text through physics-based cloth simulation. IMUdrape synthesizes motion from activity descriptions, reconstructs the target garment as a sewing pattern from photographs, places virtual IMUs at the deployment's real sensor positions, and simulates the garment with an intersection-free contact solver, so that every simulation yields paired garment and body signals. On four real datasets with clothing-mounted sensors, IMUdrape improves on existing virtual-data approaches for both activity recognition and full-body pose estimation. With one real subject, its data improve recognition macro-F1 by 7% to 46% over real data alone and by 2% to 11% over body-mounted virtual data from the same motion. When the garment is simulated on recorded motion, the pose error without any real training data falls by 30% to 33% relative to generated motion, and garment data remain 12% more accurate than body data. IMUdrape thus reduces the amount of real data that garment-mounted sensing requires.
- 2026-10-31, **3D Neural Rendering for Automated Ultrasonic Laser Scans**, Thomas Pierson et.al., Paper: [@2611.00527](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00527)
> **Abstract:** Laser doppler vibrometer (LDV) scans can be used to detect defects and cracks in manufactured parts before they are deployed. This work presents a method for reconstructing the steady-state surface wavefield of a three-dimensional part by combining automated robotic manipulation with neural rendering. A six-degree-of-freedom (6-DOF) robotic manipulator (UFactory xArm6) with an embedded piezoelectric transducer presented an acoustically excited test article to a stationary LDV at a sequence of known orientations, yielding a set of complex-valued 2D scans with poses derived analytically from the arm kinematics rather than from image-based pose estimation. These scans were used to train a novel complex-nerfacto model, a modified Neural Radiance Field (NeRF) pipeline that replaces the standard three-channel RGB output head with a two-channel output. Complex-nerfacto represents the real and imaginary velocity components of a wavefield, preserving the complex measurement without quantization. Reconstructions of a welded aluminum cylinder and a welded T-bracket were evaluated against measured scans using Z-normalized cross-correlation (ZNCC). The complex-valued pipeline achieved a mean ZNCC of 0.60 at held-out viewing angles, compared with 0.28 for a baseline RGB nerfacto model trained on rendered images of the same data. The resulting surface wavefield reconstructions supported defect detection and characterization on complex components. We identify geometry reconstruction from sparse LDV scans as the remaining obstacle to practical deployment.
- 2026-10-30, **SketchForge: Training-Free Scene-to-Vector Sketching via Decoupled Structure and Texture Optimization**, Quang-Thinh Nguyen et.al., Paper: [@2611.00253](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00253)
> **Abstract:** Converting a raster image into a resolution-independent, stroke-editable vector sketch while faithfully preserving both structural contours and tonal texture remains challenging: raster-space rendering pipelines are difficult to edit at the stroke level, while generation-from-scratch vectorization methods struggle to remain faithful to an arbitrary input image. We present SketchForge, a training-free framework that converts an input raster image into a resolution-independent SVG sketch through per-instance test-time optimization of Bezier curves, requiring no additional training or fine-tuning. Strokes are seeded via a contour and attention-guided initialization, then refined by a vector-native differentiable rendering pipeline that decouples structural contour strokes from free-form texture strokes under dedicated objectives, composited via multiply-blend layering. Extensive experiments show that SketchForge consistently outperforms existing vector-based baselines in structural fidelity and generation quality. Project page: https://khangcntn123.github.io/SketchForge
- 2026-10-27, **ActiveAlign: Aligning Egocentric Manipulation via Active Perception**, Ri-Zhao Qiu et.al., Paper: [@2610.36859](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36859)
> **Abstract:** Real-to-Sim-to-Real (Real2Sim2Real) enables structured world representations for planning, interpretability, and safe execution. Most existing approaches rely on passive perception, which creates real2sim gaps due to imperfect observation. Inspired by how humans perceive actively through actions, we propose ActiveAlign, a framework that leverages active perception to align the Real2Sim2Real gap from interactions. ActiveAlign introduces two complementary modules: (i) ActiveAlign-Waving, which performs hand-eye calibration by exploiting active hand motions and differentiable rendering, and (ii) ActiveAlign-Touching, which refines object alignment through tactile feedback to achieve millimeter-level precision. Together, these capabilities enable strong real2sim alignment. We demonstrate that ActiveAlign-Waving can be used to curate simulation environments for open-sourced uncalibrated robot manipulation datasets, and demonstrate on a real Unitree G1 robot that ActiveAlign enables fine manipulation of millimeter-scale objects such as pressing buttons and moving sliders.
- 2026-10-26, **SceneAgent: An Agentic Pipeline from 3D and 4D Captures to Simulation Environments**, Luke Hollis et.al., Paper: [@2610.34745](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34745)
> **Abstract:** Visual captures provide detailed scene appearance, but converting them into interactive simulation environments requires object reconstruction, physical modeling, and correction of intermediate errors. We present SceneAgent, an agentic pipeline that coordinates these steps through simulator-based visual inspection and repair. The pipeline processes 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), photogrammetry, LiDAR captures, AI-generated 3DGS scenes, and 4D videos of dynamic scenes, and combines object segmentation, physical parameter estimation, part decomposition, articulation, and deformable simulation. To support policy learning, SceneAgent generates diverse object variants ("digital sisters") and scripted demonstrations. On a cluttered bin scene from GraspClutter6D, SceneAgent raises F1 at 1 cm for every coding agent with scorable outputs both with and without its skills, and its reconstructions are better than SimFoundry: all agent configurations exceed SimFoundry's F1 score, with a best of 60.75% versus 6.9%. On three dynamic scenes from 4DCodeBench, SceneAgent raises the mean dynamic IoU of Gemini 4 Argon from 0.86% to 99.09%. We further demonstrate diverse simulated interactions and preliminary policy transfer to a real robot.

## Narrative & Long-form Audio

- 2026-11-05, **FoleyForge: Free-form, Controllable, Rectified-flow Editing of Sound Effects**, Giovana Morais et.al., Paper: [@2611.08695](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08695)
> **Abstract:** We present FoleyForge, a generative model for instruction-based editing of sound effects. Given an input clip and a free-form instruction describing the desired change, it applies the edit while preserving the input's identity. A single model handles semantic, stylistic, and DSP-like changes. We introduce a data pipeline that guides an audio language model to author both DSP-based acoustic edits and similarity-preserving semantic edits, thus addressing the lack of publicly available audio editing data. Such data is then used to fine-tune a rectified-flow audio generation model, which we extend to expose attribute controls for loudness change, edit strength, and an input-derived contour envelope, each tunable via classifier-free guidance, yielding a more expressive editing model. FoleyForge achieves state-of-the-art instruction adherence at competitive audio quality and source faithfulness.
- 2026-10-30, **MFlowAudio: Efficient Text-to-Audio Synthesis via Mamba-based Stateful Flow Matching**, Hao Dai et.al., Paper: [@2610.41852](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41852)
> **Abstract:** Recent advancements in audio generation have been largely driven by Transformer-based diffusion models. However, these models suffer from quadratic complexity of self-attention, severely bottlenecking the long-form audio synthesis. To overcome this limitation, we propose MFlowAudio, a novel latent audio generation framework that synergizes the continuous-time dynamics of Flow Matching with a custom-designed TCMamba backbone. TCMamba uses an innovative dual-scan mechanism: a TimeMamba module to capture long-range causal dependencies with linear complexity, and a ChannelMamba module to model cross-channel correlations such as harmonic structures. Exploiting this structural foundation, we formulate the Stateful Flow Matching (SFM) paradigm. This framework inherently enables chunk-wise training and streaming generation, maintaining an $\mathcal{O}(1)$ caching complexity without incurring extra computational overhead. To enable fine-grained controllable synthesis, we devise a novel guidance mechanism that neutralizes vector field collisions precipitated by on-the-fly semantic transitions of prompts. Comprehensive empirical evaluations confirm that MFlowAudio yields generative fidelity on par with state-of-the-art baselines while establishing significant computational efficiency, achieving above $14.5$% acceleration in generation and facilitating perceptually seamless streaming synthesis with a constant latency of $1.8$ seconds. Demo: https://huggingface.co/spaces/mflowaudio/MFlowAudio
- 2026-10-30, **Hierarchical Dual-Graph Retrieval for Long-form Audio Reasoning**, Eunsang Lee et.al., Paper: [@2610.41390](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41390)
> **Abstract:** Large audio language models (LALMs) struggle with long-form audio reasoning, as processing entire recordings often limits to capture query-relevant content distributed across distant segments while incurring substantial computational cost. We introduce AudioHelix, a hierarchical DualGraph framework tailored for LALMs' retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems. Unlike existing text-only or non-graph RAG approaches, AudioHelix connects related textual and acoustic evidence across temporally distant segments for multi-hop reasoning. Extensive experiments on TraceAV-Bench, MMAU-Pro, and AudioMarathon demonstrate that AudioHelix consistently outperforms full-audio processing and existing RAG methods. The project site is https://github.com/ewha-mmai/audiohelix.
- 2026-10-28, **A Tutorial on Generative Models for Music Audio**, Mateusz Modrzejewski et.al., Paper: [@2610.38252](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38252)
> **Abstract:** Music audio generation spans instrumental clips, full songs with vocals, edits of existing recordings, and systems that play alongside a musician in real time. This monograph explains the methods behind these systems for readers familiar with neural-network training who are new to music generation. It introduces neural audio codecs and autoencoders, autoregressive and masked token models, diffusion and flow matching, sampling acceleration, and preference optimization in a shared notation. Worked examples connect training objectives and sampling procedures to published systems. The application sections relate the musical requirements of instrumental generation, songs, editing, and live performance to model design. Comparison tables record representations, methods, inputs, tasks, and release information documented in papers and official sources. Symbolic composition, speech, and sound effects are included where they connect to music audio. We explain how to interpret automatic metrics and learned judges, design and report listening tests, and assess evidence about data provenance, memorization, attribution, and output detection. Selected legal decisions and disputes illustrate the implications for training and release. The reviewed systems combine established generative paradigms with new representations, planning stages, and post-training procedures. Differences in data, scale, and evaluation limit what cross-system comparisons can establish about the sources of reported gains.
- 2026-10-27, **Partially Edited Audio: Generation, Defense, and Open Problems**, Lin Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2610.37640](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37640)
> **Abstract:** Recent advances in generative audio models facilitate increasingly flexible and selective editing of linguistic content and paralinguistic, extralinguistic, and other attributes. This paradigm, named partially edited audio, differs from full audio generation as only portions of the real recording are edited. This technique can serve both beneficial and harmful applications. We therefore present a concise survey from both generation and defense perspectives, focusing on audio, as a contribution to the ICASSP 2027 special session entitled "Partially Edited Media: Perspectives from Synthesis and Defense." We first define partially edited audio and argue that what counts as ``fake'' or harmful is an application-dependent judgment, not a fixed property of the edit. We then survey audio editing by editing target, approaches, and evaluation, and then defense methods by detection, localization, diarization, and interpretability, summarizing representative benchmarks and toolkits. Finally, we discuss future directions and open problems.
- 2026-10-27, **TAC2: Timestamped Audio Captioning as composable functions**, Swapnil Bhosale et.al., Paper: [@2610.36568](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36568)
> **Abstract:** Real-world audio applications require precise, queryable representations grounded in physical time. Yet modern Large Audio-Language Models (LALMs) increasingly favor conversational generation, producing free-form descriptions that conflate acoustic perception with higher-level reasoning and lack precise temporal grounding. We introduce TAC2, a foundation model that casts timestamped audio captioning as composable functions to construct granular, speaker-attributed acoustic timelines directly from in-the-wild audio. To avoid prohibitively expensive manual annotation, we programmatically fuse complementary predictions from specialized weak teachers through time-registered IoU matching. We model the resulting multi-granularity schema of timestamped events, speaker turns, and fine-grained speech attributes using an Instruction-Input-Output (IIO) completion format, in which each task is a function of the audio, an instruction, and an optional input. A single call decodes the full description autonomously, while composing calls lets earlier outputs or external priors, such as a reference transcript, condition later ones. We further extend TAC2 with expressive speech attributes to obtain TAC2-S, using parameter-efficient specialists to elicit latent paralinguistic capabilities from the pre-trained backbone and distill them into a unified model. We validate the resulting representation in two complementary settings. Paired with frozen text-only LLMs, it yields strong spoken audio understanding across diverse reasoning backends, demonstrating the utility of decoupling fine-grained acoustic perception from downstream reasoning. Used as paired supervision for text-to-audio generation, it supports semantically and temporally grounded reconstruction, together with explicit zero-shot control over event timing, speaker attributes, and word-level emphasis.
